# ROUTER.md

This file is the first entry point for an AI agent working in this repo: it tells you what to read, what to run, and which files are canonical.

## Role split

- `ROUTER.md` = startup order and canonical entry points
- `AGENTS.md` = behavioral rules, decision quality, reversibility, blast radius, proof
- `README.md` = human-facing repo/product overview
- `ROADMAP.md` = pointer ledger of queued, current, completed, attempted, and deferred work
- `CHANGELOG.md` = the end-of-iteration running log (first-class PDDA artifact; governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md`)
- `PROJECT/**` docs = canonical execution detail for a specific effort
- `PROJECT/PDDA.md` = document contract and automation rules (incl. the CHANGELOG contract)
- `PROJECT/CONSTITUTION.md` = the policy of record: PDDA's lane and its non-negotiables (deterministic-before-LLM, verified-success-only, reversibility, local-first)
- `PROJECT/DO-NOT-BUILD.md` = the anti-scope list — product directions PDDA must not become (companion to `CONSTITUTION.md`)

## Startup sequence

1. Read `ROUTER.md` to understand the repo's operating order and canonical files. -> expect one clear next file, not a repo-wide scavenger hunt.
2. Read `AGENTS.md` before making recommendations or edits. -> expect explicit assumptions, a reversibility read on consequential changes, and verified claims only.
3. Read `ROADMAP.md` to find the active effort or parked intake. -> expect links outward to the canonical `PROJECT/**` docs; `ROADMAP.md` is a pointer ledger, not a plan body.
4. Read the linked `PROJECT/**` document that owns the work you are touching. -> expect the near-top `## Status` table to tell you what was just completed and what is next.
5. If the task touches project docs, read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and follow the PDDA contract. -> expect `PROJECT/2-WORKING` docs to have frontmatter, the exact status table, and QA gates when phased.
6. Before reporting success on code or runtime work, run `./validate.sh`. -> expect the suite to stay green; do not claim completion if it fails or was skipped.
7. Before reporting success on doc-hygiene or roadmap work, run `utils/pdda/pdda.sh run` (or the relevant `utils/pdda/pdda.sh <check>` subcommand). -> expect deterministic findings first, then any LLM review.

## Canonical rules

- Do not put phase checklists, build steps, or deep execution notes in `ROADMAP.md`.
- Every active doc in `PROJECT/2-WORKING/` must be reflected by a pointer in `ROADMAP.md` — a one-line ledger entry that links it. A working doc that should not appear opts out with `roadmap_exempt: true` in its frontmatter. Enforced by `utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage`; governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "ROADMAP.md contract".
- Every captured GitHub issue doc in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` must also be parked in `ROADMAP.md` as a one-line queue entry immediately at intake, then promoted or removed later. Enforced by `utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage`; governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake" + "ROADMAP.md contract".
- Do not create a second competing plan when a canonical `PROJECT/**` doc already exists.
- Issue-first: any change beyond a **2–3 line** fix opens a GitHub issue first, then a pointer doc **named after the issue** (`GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md`, e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md`), and that capture is **parked in `ROADMAP.md` immediately** before execution begins. The issue is the signal stream; the pointer doc is the execution surface of record. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt. Governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake".
- Do not override deterministic PDDA findings with prose.
- Do not report a win you did not verify with the relevant script or test.
- Update `CHANGELOG.md` at the end of each iteration; its governance lives in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` — do not re-specify CHANGELOG rules in `AGENTS.md` or elsewhere.

## Command rails

For repo correctness:

```bash
./validate.sh
```

For document hygiene:

```bash
utils/pdda/pdda.sh run
```

For targeted PDDA debugging (subcommands of the single dispatcher):

```bash
utils/pdda/pdda.sh frontmatter
utils/pdda/pdda.sh status-table
utils/pdda/pdda.sh hardcoded-paths
utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap
utils/pdda/pdda.sh roadmap-coverage
utils/pdda/pdda.sh changelog
utils/pdda/pdda.sh stale
utils/pdda/pdda.sh issue-doc-sync   # warn-only: flags 2-WORKING/GH-*.md docs drifted from their GitHub issue state
utils/pdda/pdda.sh doc-ready        # LLM readiness review — set PDDA_LLM_BIN (codex/claude/agy) for recommendations, else it self-skips
```

## Routing hints

- If the task is about current priorities or active work, start in `ROADMAP.md`, then follow the linked `PROJECT/**` doc.
- If the task is about fresh GitHub intake or duplicate-prevention, start in `ROADMAP.md`'s queue, then follow the linked `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` capture doc.
- If the task is about document quality, active-doc lifecycle, roadmap sprawl, or automation policy, start in `PROJECT/PDDA.md`.
- If the task is about the CHANGELOG, provenance, or end-of-iteration logging, the governance is in `PROJECT/PDDA.md` (the "CHANGELOG.md — end-of-iteration record" contract).
- If the task is about the `tick` runtime, event projection, or multi-agent coordination kernel, start in `README.md`, then `bin/`, `src/`, `test/`, and the active project doc.
- If the task is about the **Aider ↔ OpenRouter** turn-taker lane (`relay-automation/aider-turn.sh` — an OpenAI-standard build lane discrete from Codex; `AIDER_MODEL`/`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `--builder aider`), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-77-AIDER-OPENROUTER-LANE.md`. The shim owns the tick token ops (Aider can't run shell mid-turn), asserts token ownership before launching Aider, and runs Aider `--no-auto-commits` (the harness commits).
- If the task is about running, driving, or reviewing via the relay (`relay-automation/` — `relay-drive.sh`, `poll.sh`, the turn shims, `marathon*.sh`), **invoke the `relay-xyz` skill first — do not improvise the handoff or hand-roll a harness from `ls relay-automation/`.** The skill owns the locator, sandbox rules, exit codes, and the safety boundary; a `PreToolUse` guard (`relay-automation/hooks/relay-xyz-guard.sh`) blocks driving a harness driver before the skill is loaded. For the two live-Claude-windows, same-machine duel recipe (Reporter↔Maintainer with a human go-gate), the copy-paste form is [relay-automation/DUELING-CLAUDES.md](relay-automation/DUELING-CLAUDES.md).
- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
- Issue-first SOP: any change beyond a 2–3 line fix (and every project plan) opens a GitHub issue *first*, then gets a pointer doc named after the issue at `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md` — e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md` — and that capture is parked in the `ROADMAP.md` queue immediately (format + lifecycle owned by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake"), following the normal `1-INBOX` → `2-WORKING` flow. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt and commit directly.

# Git Worktree Safety Guide for Agents

> **Purpose:** Prevent destructive footguns when scripting with Git worktrees.  
> **Scope:** Shell scripts, CI pipelines, and agent workflows that create, manage, or clean up worktrees.

---

## 1. The "rm -rf worktree path" trap

**Anti-pattern:** Deleting a worktree by just removing its directory.

```bash
# WRONG — leaves stale metadata in .git/worktrees/
rm -rf ../feature-branch

# Also WRONG — git still thinks the worktree exists
git worktree remove ../feature-branch  # fails: "not a working tree"
```

**Why it's dangerous:** Git maintains metadata in `.git/worktrees/<name>/` and in a `.git` file inside the worktree. If you `rm -rf` the directory, you get:
- Orphaned metadata polluting your repo
- The branch may still be checked out according to git, blocking operations
- `.git/worktrees/<name>/index` can grow large and never gets cleaned

**Correct approach:**
```bash
# Always use git worktree remove
git worktree remove ../feature-branch

# If the directory is already gone, let git reconcile its own metadata —
# don't hand-delete .git/worktrees/<name> yourself:
git worktree prune

# If the worktree still exists but was moved/relinked and git can't find it,
# `repair` (Git 2.29+) is the documented fix, not manual surgery on .git/worktrees/:
git worktree repair ../feature-branch
```
Manual `rm -rf .git/worktrees/<name>` is a last resort for a clearly corrupt admin
stub that `prune`/`repair` won't touch — not the normal cleanup path.

---

## 2. Scripting `git worktree add` without failure handling

**Anti-pattern:** Assuming `git worktree add` succeeds.

```bash
git worktree add ../hotfix hotfix-branch
cd ../hotfix || exit 1
# ... do work ...
```

**Why it's dangerous:**
- Branch might already be checked out in another worktree (git refuses with "already checked out")
- Path might already exist
- Disk might be full
- Detached HEAD might not be what you expected

**Defensive version:**
```bash
if ! git worktree add ../hotfix hotfix-branch 2>/dev/null; then
    echo "Worktree creation failed — branch may already be checked out or path exists" >&2
    exit 1
fi
```

---

## 3. Trap cleaning worktrees with `rm -rf` and relative paths

**Anti-pattern:** The sibling of the `mktemp` bug — cleaning worktrees in traps.

```bash
WORKTREE="../feature-$(date +%s)"
git worktree add "$WORKTREE" feature-branch
trap 'rm -rf "$WORKTREE"' EXIT
```

**Why it's dangerous:**
- If `git worktree add` fails and `WORKTREE` is empty/malformed, a quoted `rm -rf "$WORKTREE"` errors on an empty string (`rm: missing operand`) rather than silently targeting cwd — but an *unquoted* `rm -rf $WORKTREE` word-splits an empty value to zero arguments, which for GNU `rm` is also a no-op/error, NOT an implicit `.`. The real risk isn't a specific "resolves to cwd" mechanism at all: it's that an unvalidated variable in a destructive trap can hold anything (a partial path, a stray `*`, a value from a prior failed `cd`) by the time `EXIT` fires, and nothing between assignment and the trap firing re-checks it
- If the script `cd`s into the worktree, the relative path `../` now points somewhere else
- `rm -rf` leaves stale metadata in `.git/worktrees/`

**Defensive version:**
```bash
# NOTE: unlike mktemp, git worktree add does NOT expand "XXXX" into a random
# suffix — that string would be used verbatim as the path. Build the unique
# path yourself before calling git, and don't rely on parsing git's output
# (--quiet suppresses exactly the text a naive script would try to awk out of it).
WORKTREE="$(pwd)/../feature-$$-$(date +%s)"
git worktree add "$WORKTREE" feature-branch || { echo "Worktree creation failed" >&2; exit 1; }
WORKTREE="$(cd "$WORKTREE" && pwd -P)"  # canonicalize AFTER validation

cleanup() {
    # --force here is NOT the §12 anti-pattern: this worktree was just created by THIS script for a
    # throwaway purpose and is being torn down in its own exit trap, not force-removed out from under
    # someone else's uncommitted work. §12's warning is about scripts reaching for --force to silence
    # an error on a worktree they don't own/didn't create.
    git worktree remove --force "$WORKTREE" 2>/dev/null || true
    git worktree prune 2>/dev/null || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT
```

---

## 4. Moving/renaming worktree directories outside of git

**Anti-pattern:** Using `mv` to relocate a worktree.

```bash
mv ../feature-branch ../feature-branch-old
```

**Why it's dangerous:** The `.git` file inside the worktree contains an absolute or relative path back to the main repo. Moving it breaks that link. Git now can't find the worktree, and `git worktree remove` fails.

**Correct approach:**
```bash
# git worktree move shipped in Git 2.17.0 — use it instead of mv
git worktree move ../feature-branch ../feature-branch-renamed

# Pre-2.17: remove and re-add
git worktree remove ../feature-branch
git worktree add ../feature-branch-renamed feature-branch

# If a worktree (or the main worktree) was ALREADY moved outside git's
# knowledge — e.g. via `mv`, a backup restore, or a renamed parent dir — the
# documented fix is `repair` (Git 2.29+), not manual .git-file surgery:
git worktree repair ../feature-branch-renamed
```

---

## 5. Assuming `main` (or any shared branch) is free for checkout

**Anti-pattern:** `git worktree add` for a branch that's already checked out elsewhere.

```bash
# Script adds a worktree for "main" to run tests
git worktree add ../main-worktree main
```

**Why it's dangerous:** If any other worktree already has `main` checked out, this fails. This is especially problematic in CI or multi-session environments.

**Defensive version:**
```bash
# Use a unique branch name or detached HEAD
git worktree add --detach ../test-run-$$ main

# Or check first — parse --porcelain, not human-readable output. The plain
# `git worktree list` format is not a stable API and grep can false-match on
# pathnames that happen to contain "[main]"-like substrings.
if git worktree list --porcelain | grep -qx 'branch refs/heads/main'; then
    echo "main is already checked out in another worktree" >&2
    exit 1
fi
```

---

## 6. Garbage collection while worktrees exist

**Anti-pattern:** Running aggressive GC without considering worktrees.

```bash
git gc --aggressive --prune=now
```

**Why it's dangerous:**
- Worktrees share the same object database, and (with the exception of
  `refs/bisect`, `refs/worktree`, and `refs/rewritten`) the same refs — modern
  Git *is* worktree-aware and does scan all registered worktrees' refs/logs
  before pruning, so "gc can't see another worktree's refs" is not the
  mechanism
- The real documented risk is **concurrency**: `--prune=now` disables the
  normal grace-period safety margin, so if another process (a build in a
  linked worktree, a concurrent commit) creates an object that isn't
  referenced by a ref yet, `--prune=now` can delete it out from under that
  process — a race, not a worktree-visibility gap
- A secondary, worktree-specific risk: if a worktree directory was manually
  `rm -rf`'d without `git worktree prune`, its stale `.git/worktrees/<name>/`
  admin entry can leave git's bookkeeping out of sync with reality until
  pruned

**Defensive approach:**
```bash
# Always list worktrees before GC to understand what's shared
git worktree list

# Avoid --prune=now while any worktree might be mid-write (build, commit, checkout)
# Or avoid --prune=now entirely
git gc --auto  # conservative, safe
```

---

## 7. Deleting the main worktree's `.git` directory

**Anti-pattern:** Treating the main `.git` directory as just another git database.

```bash
# Thinking you're cleaning up an old clone
rm -rf .git
```

**Why it's dangerous:** All linked worktrees reference the main repo's object database via their `.git` files. Deleting the main `.git` irrecoverably breaks every linked worktree.

**Real-world scenario:** You have 3 worktrees off a main checkout. Someone decides to "clean up" by deleting the main checkout folder. Now all 3 worktrees are orphaned with no object database, and even `git log` fails.

**Precaution:**
```bash
# Before removing any repo, check if it's the primary for worktrees
git worktree list
# If other worktrees reference this one's objects, don't delete .git
```

---

## 8. Scripts that `cd` into a worktree then use relative paths back

# Guiding Principles

North star for **xyz-3-agents-swarm**, the multi-agent coordination harness behind the `tick` event-log kernel and `relay-automation/` relay stack. When a choice is unclear, the option that keeps agents synchronized, contained, and verifiable — without leaking or destroying work — wins. AGENTS.md is the behavioral playbook; ROUTER.md is the entry-point map; this is the *why*.

## Purpose

`tick` coordinates Claude Code, Codex, and agy (Antigravity CLI) on the same branch without collision: a shared local event log under `.tick/events/`, claims serialized by `O_EXCL` locks, and a `Marathon` harness that chains multi-phase build→review cycles from a `MARATHON.yaml`. The relay layer (`relay-automation/`) drives headless turns, isolates agent writes to worktrees, and enforces an allowlist so no headless agent destroys work it didn't intend to touch. The goal: a multi-agent swarm safe enough to run against a real external codebase and correct enough that its output is worth shipping.

## The quality bar

Every agent turn is a signal. A turn is high-quality only when it is all four:

- **Attested** — carries its receipts: source, evidence, confidence. Never a bare verdict. A relay review names which claim is wrong and why; a build turn names the seam it touched.
- **Relevant** — ranked, not dumped. Volume is not value. One real bug beats five nits and a phantom.
- **Fresh** — current, not stale. A turn that reads a stale `STATE.md` or misses an epoch fence is wrong by construction.
- **Structured** — one shape, clean for the operator to read and for downstream agents to feed on.

Fail a pillar, and the turn, feature, or relay review isn't done.

## How it's built

1. **Coordination is local-transport only.** `.tick/events/` is the shared bus; claims resolve from there, not from a remote. No per-event push/fetch; no remote dependency at runtime. A coordination primitive that reaches out is a coordination primitive that can fail or leak.

2. **One canonical event log; every surface is a projection.** `tick` accretes events; `STATE.md` is the current projection. Reads go through the projection; writes go through a `claim/take/scope/done` verb. Nothing canonical lives in two places where it can drift. An agent that hard-codes state outside `.tick/` is creating drift.

3. **Containment is non-negotiable.** A headless turn must not: self-commit mid-turn, orphan a peer's concurrent commit, or write outside its allowlist. The allowlist, worktree isolation, and commit-bypass guard exist because a driven agent will do all three if unconstrained — not hypothetically, but as documented live incidents (GH-13, GH-14, GH-17). New relay paths must clear the containment bar before they ship.

4. **Skill-first; never improvise the harness.** The `relay-xyz` skill owns the locator, sandbox rules, exit codes, and the safety boundary. A session that improvises those from `ls relay-automation/` silently skips the skill's safety layer. The `PreToolUse` guard (`relay-automation/hooks/relay-xyz-guard.sh`) enforces this by blocking driver calls before the skill loads. Add capabilities to the skill; do not work around it.

5. **Adversarially proven before commercially viable.** The harness exists to run against real codebases. Features in the adversarial-hardening track (epoch fencing, chaos suite, cross-repo E2E) must be verified to survive deliberate abuse — stale writers, zombie claims, macOS case-sensitivity, concurrent peer commits — not just the happy path. A feature that clears the happy path and skips chaos is half-done.

6. **Build durable, not band-aid.** Durable means it removes the root cause and the next planned change builds on it — not a patch torn out when the obvious next feature lands. A band-aid is wasted work unless a demo strictly needs one, and a demo band-aid is tagged for removal so it isn't silently inherited.

7. **Least code that clears the bar.** Node standard library only — no deps, no lockfile; the repo ships no root manifest. Prefer reusing or extending what exists; the smallest change that stays correct, contained, and durable wins. Net-new code is a cost to justify. Deleting code counts as progress.

8. **Honest; the operator decides.** Surface what failed and why — never mask a stall as success or an escalation as a stall. A headless turn self-repairs within a bounded exit-code menu (`exit 3` stall, `exit 4` escalated-by-design, `exit 6` containment revert), then stops; it never loops forever or silently swallows an error. Destructive actions require explicit authorization.

9. **Docs are resumable runtime state (PDDA).** Agent work is stoppable, resumable, and handed off from `PROJECT/**` alone — ROUTER points, project docs hold detail, CHANGELOG logs dated outcomes. ROADMAP.md is a pointer/ledger only; execution detail lives in the linked `PROJECT/**` doc. If reality and the docs disagree, the docs are the bug.

10. **Done means verified.** "Done" is `validate.sh` green, the relevant PDDA checks passing, and any relay review returning `Approved` — not work that looks finished. An unverified success claim is itself a low-quality signal.

11. **Issue-first; every non-trivial change has a signal stream.** Any change beyond a 2–3 line fix opens a GitHub issue first, then gets a `GH-<number>` in-repo pointer doc, then lands. The issue is the machine-queryable signal stream; the `PROJECT/**` doc is the execution surface of record. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt.

12. **Independent Verification (Separated Grading)** — The agent that produces a turn must not be the sole grader of its own quality. Verification must be performed by an independent deterministic check or a separate reviewing agent before the lock releases. Applies to: the relay's structural block validator (`bin/validate-relay-block` — Phase 1 of GH-21), consult-verify diversity (Phase 3), and any other post-generation quality gate.

## Applying this

Adding a feature or weighing a tradeoff, ask: *does this keep agents coordinated without collision, contained within their scope, and verifiable to an outside observer? And is "done" provable by running `validate.sh`?* If any answer is no, reconsider.

---

## Conventions

### Strict-mode policy (bash `set -e`)

Strict mode is **per-subsystem, not repo-wide** (GH-110 P3b). The split is deliberate:

- **`relay-automation/` drivers and turn shims run `set -euo pipefail`.** They orchestrate risky,
  multi-step, containment-sensitive turns where a silently-ignored failure can commit off-lane or
  orphan a peer. Abort-on-error (`-e`) is the correct default there.
- **`utils/` analysis tools (`pdda/*`, `marathon-plan.sh`, `swarm-preflight.sh`) run `set -uo pipefail`
  or `set -u`, deliberately *without* `-e`.** These are long single-pass scripts whose normal control
  flow includes many expected-nonzero probes (`git rev-parse`, `gh` lookups, `grep` misses). Under
  `-e` a benign "no match" would abort the whole run, so they set `-u` (catch unset vars) + explicit
  per-call error handling instead. This is an exemption, not an oversight.

Every currently `-e`-exempt script carries a one-line `# strict-mode: -e exempt — …` header next to
its `set -` line so the exemption is self-documenting. New scripts default to `set -euo pipefail`
unless they fit the analysis-tool profile above, in which case they add the exemption header.

---

## Appendix: AI Doc Review Heuristics

When reviewing any repo doc (roadmap entries, plans, architecture notes, audits, task writeups), apply these. Priority: containment > coordination correctness > signal quality > implementation speed and operator friction.

**Heuristics**

1. **Containment preserved?** Any headless path that could self-commit, touch off-allowlist files, or orphan a peer commit without an explicit containment argument → reject or escalate.
2. **Skill-first respected?** Any plan that bypasses `relay-xyz` or improvises the harness from scratch without the skill layer → reject. Add to the skill instead.
3. **Coordination through the event log?** Reads/writes to shared state route through `tick` verbs; hard-coded state outside `.tick/` needs explicit justification.
4. **Done verifiable?** Names runnable gates (`validate.sh`, specific tests, `utils/pdda/pdda.sh run`). None = low-quality signal.
5. **Drift reduced, not created?** No duplicated docs, no execution detail in ROADMAP.md, no reinventing a path the event-log contract already documents.
6. **Next action singular?** One explicit next step, not buried in prose; status cells non-empty.
7. **Operator control explicit?** No silent retry, no auto-repair outside the bounded exit-code menu, no masked failure; destructive ops surface before executing.
8. **Four pillars pass?** Each turn/output is Attested, Relevant, Fresh, Structured. Fail one → not done.

**Tie-breakers**

- **Containment vs speed:** choose containment; flag friction as a design question, not a shortcut.
- **New relay path vs reuse:** extend the existing skill and harness over forking a parallel path; if the harness can't accommodate it, surface the gap.
- **Ambitious vs resumable:** a shorter plan an agent can resume cold beats a comprehensive one that buries state in prose.

**Reject or escalate when**

- A headless path has no allowlist, no worktree isolation, and no commit-bypass guard — and the doc doesn't justify why.
- "Done" has no runnable verification step.
- Adding a new relay lane requires editing the event-log kernel or the `tick` verb schema without a decision record under `decisions/`.
- Hardcoded absolute paths, silent destructive operations, or opaque epoch-fence assumptions.
- ROADMAP.md would need execution detail to make the plan legible.

- **GH-118 · Make Aider edit formats more forgiving for OpenRouter models** 🆕 **captured 2026-07-03 · rated · live-confirmed** — Aider natively uses a 'whole' or 'udiff' edit format depending on the model, but unknown OpenRouter models (like GLM-5.2) often default to formats they don't strictly follow (e.g. outputting standard diffs instead of 'whole' file), causing Aider to fail with 'no tracked changes'. **2026-07-03 live tests confirmed the diagnosis and the fix on two models:** GLM-5.2 (first attempt: no edit emitted at all, just chat; retry with `AIDER_FLAGS=--edit-format diff` produced valid SEARCH/REPLACE) and Nemotron Ultra 3 free tier (same 'whole' default — zero nvidia/nemotron entries anywhere in Aider's model-settings.yml or heuristic chain — failed by emitting a raw unified-diff hunk, reproducing the original bug report almost exactly). Revised fix: document `AIDER_FLAGS=--edit-format diff` (already a passthrough) rather than adding a new `AIDER_EDIT_FORMAT` var; maintain a running known-model compat note. cx/risk/eff 2/1/2. → [GH-118-AIDER-OPENROUTER-FORMAT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-118-AIDER-OPENROUTER-FORMAT.md) · [#118](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/118)

- **Gate design · convergence gates that pin a *worse* code shape** 🐞 — the KWFS `#70` gate statically extracts a literal `return array(...)` from *each* of three formatter methods and asserts equality, which makes the issue's intended fix (one canonical method + delegate/delete the duplicates) **fail the gate** — the Maintainer was forced to keep three duplicated-but-identical literals, divergence the gate calls "converged." Generalizes: static-extraction needle gates can pin a less-maintainable shape than the refactor they exist to enforce. Fix direction: assert runtime output-key equality (or canonical + delegation), not three literals. → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
- **Orchestration · in-loop gate verification must run sandbox-off** 🟡 — an orchestrator/watcher re-running a target gate (`bash tests/run.sh`) under the Claude Bash sandbox **false-fails** (PHP can't create lock files → bogus "syntax errors"); the peer's un-sandboxed run is authoritative. Handle: in-loop gate self-checks run sandbox-off, or trust the peer's run + spot-confirm sandbox-off (memory: `codex-cli-needs-sandbox-disabled` family). → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)

- **GH-160 · codex CLI intermittently reports off-lane/unwritable target files inside isolated worktrees (false containment violations)** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-07-07 (`43f933c`)** — root-caused via a debug-mantra session, NOT a codex reliability issue: `codex-turn.sh`/`agy-turn.sh`/`claude-turn.sh` each default their own `TURN_ROOT` to their script's parent directory, since `marathon-drive.sh`/`relay-drive.sh` never export `CODEX_TURN_ROOT`/`AGY_TURN_ROOT`. For a vendored `<repo>/.xyz/relay-automation/*` install, that default resolves to `<repo>/.xyz`, not `<repo>` — anchoring containment at the wrong root for every codex/agy turn against a vendored repo. Explains every failure mode seen firing sleuth-app's lanes this session: worktree-mode off-lane reverts (the seed check resolved artifacts under `.xyz/`, found nothing, and deleted the real files from the worktree — codex's "worktree doesn't contain my files" complaint was true, not a hallucination) and non-worktree-mode rejection of the relay file's own turn-append. Fix: `rtl_init` now unconditionally collapses `RTL_ROOT` to its repo's toplevel whenever it's a subdirectory of that repo, using a string-based `--show-prefix` strip (not `--show-toplevel`'s physical path, which would reintroduce the `/var` vs `/private/var` mismatch GH-51 already had to avoid). New regression case in `test/relay-target-root.sh`, confirmed failing without the fix / passing with it.      1	---
     2	name: ate
     3	description: "ATE (Automated Testing Environment): drive long-running (2-3hr+) unattended variation-test suites against the Aider, OpenRouter, and GLM 5.2 pipeline using a local Gemma model in LM Studio as the worker, with periodic frontier-model check-ins for drift control, ending in a single triaged GitHub issue. Use this whenever the user wants to \"run variation tests overnight/for hours\", \"debug a bunch of small Aider bugs\", \"test Aider flag combinations\", \"fuzz the Aider harness\", mentions LM Studio + Gemma as a local test driver, wants test failures rolled up into one GH issue triaged by severity, or invokes ATE by name. Also trigger if the user asks to set up a check-in loop between a local model and a frontier model for a long-running task."
     4	---
     5	
     6	# ATE — Automated Testing Environment (Gemma-Driven Aider Variation Tester)
     7	
     8	Local Gemma (via LM Studio) runs many Aider command variations unattended for hours,
     9	logs every result as structured JSON, and a frontier model (you, Claude) checks in
    10	every ~5 minutes to catch drift/looping. At the end, results roll up into one
    11	GitHub issue, triaged by severity.
    12	
    13	## Architecture (linear)
    14	
    15	1. **Gemma (LM Studio, local)** — worker. Picks the next untested variation, runs
    16	   `aider --model openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.2 ...` as a subprocess, classifies the result.
    17	2. **error_log.jsonl** — the single source of truth. Append-only. Every variation
    18	   writes one line here regardless of pass/fail.
    19	3. **control.json** — the only channel Claude writes to. Gemma polls it before each
    20	   iteration. `{"action": "continue"}` / `{"action": "abort", "reason": "..."}`.
    21	4. **Claude (you)** — supervisor. Every ~5 min, run `checkin.py`, read the summary,
    22	   decide continue/abort, write `control.json` if aborting.
    23	5. **compile_issue.py** — run once at the end (or on abort) to group `error_log.jsonl`
    24	   by severity/signature and open one GitHub issue via `gh issue create`.
    25	
    26	## Quick start (do this first)
    27	
    28	```bash
    29	# 1. Install
    30	bash install.sh                     # copies this skill to ~/.claude/skills/
    31	
    32	# 2. In LM Studio: load a Gemma 4 model (31B Dense recommended), start the
    33	#    Local Server (Developer tab -> Start Server). Default: http://localhost:1234/v1
    34	#    Note the exact model identifier shown in LM Studio's server log/model list.
    35	
    36	# 3. Set OpenRouter key for the Aider side of the pipeline being tested
    37	export OPENROUTER_API_KEY="sk-or-v1-..."
    38	
    39	# 4. Point run_variations.py at a scratch git repo (never your real repo)
    40	cd ~/scratch/aider-test-repo && git init -q
    41	
    42	# 5. Kick off the run (defaults to 3 hours, edit variations.yaml first)
    43	python3 ~/.claude/skills/ate/scripts/run_variations.py \
    44	  --repo . \
    45	  --variations ~/.claude/skills/ate/variations.example.yaml \
    46	  --lmstudio-model "gemma-4-31b-instruct" \
    47	  --minutes 180 &
    48	```
    49	
    50	Then, on your (Claude's) side, every ~5 minutes:
    51	
    52	```bash
    53	python3 ~/.claude/skills/ate/scripts/checkin.py --tail 20
    54	```
    55	
    56	Read the printed summary. If it looks healthy, do nothing (Gemma keeps polling
    57	`control.json`, sees no abort, continues). If you see drift (see "What counts as
    58	drift" below), abort the run — `checkin.py --abort "reason"` does this for you in
    59	one call. There is no "redirect" action; the only control signals are `continue`
    60	and `abort`.
    61	
    62	When the run ends (time limit, abort, or `variations.yaml` exhausted):
    63	
    64	```bash
    65	python3 ~/.claude/skills/ate/scripts/compile_issue.py \
    66	  --log error_log.jsonl --repo OWNER/REPO
    67	```
    68	
    69	This opens one GH issue with a severity-sorted checklist. Requires `gh auth status`
    70	to already be logged in.
    71	
    72	## What counts as drift (abort triggers)
    73	
    74	- 3+ consecutive iterations with the **same** `likely_cause` signature — Gemma is
    75	  stuck re-testing the same bug instead of moving to the next variation
    76	- Gemma marks something `pass` but the raw Aider stdout/stderr in the same record
    77	  contains a traceback or non-zero exit code — classification is unreliable, stop
    78	  and inspect
    79	- No new lines appended to `error_log.jsonl` for 2+ check-in cycles — the worker
    80	  has hung (check the LM Studio server and the `aider` subprocess)
    81	- Wall-clock time on a single variation exceeds `--per-variation-timeout` (default
    82	  180s) — treat as a hang, not a slow test; `run_variations.py` kills it and logs
    83	  it as `severity: high, category: timeout` automatically
    84	
    85	## variations.yaml format
    86	
    87	Grid of Aider flags to combine (see `variations.example.yaml`). Each combination
    88	becomes one subprocess call. Keep the task `message` short and deterministic (a
    89	fixed small task like "add a docstring to foo()") so failures are attributable to
    90	the *pipeline* (Aider/OpenRouter/GLM plumbing), not to task ambiguity.
    91	
    92	```yaml
    93	model: openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.2
    94	edit_formats: [diff, whole, udiff]
    95	map_tokens: [0, 1024, 4096]
    96	auto_commits: [true, false]
    97	message: "Add a one-line docstring to the function `foo` in sample.py"
    98	per_variation_timeout_seconds: 180
    99	```
   100	
   101	## Severity rubric (used by compile_issue.py)
   102	
   103	| Severity | Definition |
   104	|---|---|
   105	| critical | Aider process crashes, non-zero exit with traceback, or corrupts the git working tree |
   106	| high | Wrong/no edit applied, OpenRouter auth or routing failure, timeout/hang |
   107	| medium | Edit applied but malformed (bad diff format, partial file write) |
   108	| low | Cosmetic — extra output noise, formatting drift, slow-but-succeeded |
   109	
   110	## Files in this skill
   111	
   112	- `scripts/run_variations.py` — the Gemma-driven worker loop (long-running)
   113	- `scripts/checkin.py` — Claude-side supervisor: summarize + optionally abort
   114	- `scripts/compile_issue.py` — rolls up `error_log.jsonl` into one GH issue
   115	- `variations.example.yaml` — starter grid, copy and edit per pipeline under test
   116	- `install.sh` — copies this skill folder to `~/.claude/skills/`
   117	
   118	## Notes / gotchas
   119	
   120	- LM Studio's OpenAI-compatible endpoint ignores auth — no API key needed locally,
   121	  but you must pass the exact model name LM Studio reports (`/v1/models` will list it).
   122	- Aider's OpenRouter model string needs the `openrouter/` prefix on top of the
   123	  OpenRouter slug itself: `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.2` — the bare slug alone will fail.
   124	- Gemma 4's training cutoff is Jan 2025 — if Aider's CLI flags have changed since,
   125	  Gemma may generate stale flag names. `run_variations.py` only ever calls flags
   126	  from `variations.yaml` (never lets Gemma invent flags), which sidesteps this.
   127	- Run everything against a disposable scratch repo. `run_variations.py` does not
   128	  do any cleanup/reset of the target repo between variations by default.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""
     3	Gemma-driven Aider variation tester.
     4	
     5	Runs a grid of Aider CLI variations against a target pipeline (Aider -> OpenRouter
     6	-> GLM 5.2 by default), asks a local Gemma model (served by LM Studio) to classify
     7	each result, and appends one JSON record per variation to error_log.jsonl.
     8	
     9	Polls control.json before every iteration so a supervising frontier model (Claude)
    10	can abort or redirect the run mid-flight.
    11	
    12	Requires: `pip install requests pyyaml`
    13	LM Studio: Developer tab -> Start Server (default http://localhost:1234/v1)
    14	"""
    15	import argparse
    16	import itertools
    17	import json
    18	import subprocess
    19	import time
    20	import sys
    21	from pathlib import Path
    22	
    23	import requests
    24	import yaml
    25	
    26	CLASSIFY_PROMPT = """You are triaging the output of a test run for an Aider -> OpenRouter -> \
    27	GLM 5.2 coding pipeline. Given the command, exit code, and truncated stdout/stderr below, \
    28	classify the result. Respond with ONLY a JSON object, no prose, no markdown fences:
    29	
    30	{{"status": "pass" or "fail",
    31	  "severity": "critical" | "high" | "medium" | "low" | "none",
    32	  "category": short slug e.g. "crash" | "auth_failure" | "bad_diff" | "timeout" | "no_edit" | "ok",
    33	  "likely_cause": one short sentence}}
    34	
    35	COMMAND: {command}
    36	EXIT_CODE: {exit_code}
    37	STDOUT_TAIL:
    38	{stdout}
    39	STDERR_TAIL:
    40	{stderr}
    41	"""
    42	
    43	
    44	def ask_gemma(base_url: str, model: str, prompt: str, timeout: int = 60) -> dict:
    45	    try:
    46	        resp = requests.post(
    47	            f"{base_url}/chat/completions",
    48	            json={
    49	                "model": model,
    50	                "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
    51	                "temperature": 0.1,
    52	                "max_tokens": 300,
    53	            },
    54	            timeout=timeout,
    55	        )
    56	        resp.raise_for_status()
    57	        text = resp.json()["choices"][0]["message"]["content"].strip()
    58	    except requests.exceptions.RequestException as e:
    59	        return {
    60	            "status": "unknown",
    61	            "severity": "medium",
    62	            "category": "classification_request_error",
    63	            "likely_cause": f"LM Studio request failed: {e}",
    64	        }
    65	    # Gemma sometimes wraps JSON in fences despite instructions; strip them.
    66	    text = text.strip("`")
    67	    if text.startswith("json"):
    68	        text = text[4:].strip()
    69	    try:
    70	        return json.loads(text)
    71	    except json.JSONDecodeError:
    72	        return {
    73	            "status": "unknown",
    74	            "severity": "medium",
    75	            "category": "classification_parse_error",
    76	            "likely_cause": f"Gemma returned non-JSON: {text[:200]}",
    77	        }
    78	
    79	
    80	def build_variations(grid: dict) -> list[dict]:
    81	    keys = ["edit_formats", "map_tokens", "auto_commits"]
    82	    values = [grid[k] for k in keys]
    83	    combos = []
    84	    for edit_format, map_tokens, auto_commits in itertools.product(*values):
    85	        combos.append(
    86	            {
    87	                "edit_format": edit_format,
    88	                "map_tokens": map_tokens,
    89	                "auto_commits": auto_commits,
    90	            }
    91	        )
    92	    return combos
    93	
    94	
    95	def run_aider(repo: str, model: str, variation: dict, message: str, timeout: int):
    96	    cmd = [
    97	        "aider",
    98	        "--model", model,
    99	        "--edit-format", variation["edit_format"],
   100	        "--map-tokens", str(variation["map_tokens"]),
   101	        "--yes",
   102	        "--no-stream",
   103	        "--message", message,
   104	    ]
   105	    if not variation["auto_commits"]:
   106	        cmd.append("--no-auto-commits")
   107	
   108	    start = time.time()
   109	    try:
   110	        proc = subprocess.run(
   111	            cmd, cwd=repo, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout
   112	        )
   113	        return {
   114	            "command": " ".join(cmd),
   115	            "exit_code": proc.returncode,
   116	            "stdout": proc.stdout[-4000:],
   117	            "stderr": proc.stderr[-4000:],
   118	            "wall_seconds": round(time.time() - start, 1),
   119	            "timed_out": False,
   120	        }
   121	    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
   122	        return {
   123	            "command": " ".join(cmd),
   124	            "exit_code": None,
   125	            "stdout": (e.stdout or "")[-4000:] if e.stdout else "",
   126	            "stderr": (e.stderr or "")[-4000:] if e.stderr else "",
   127	            "wall_seconds": round(time.time() - start, 1),
   128	            "timed_out": True,
   129	        }
   130	
   131	
   132	def check_control(control_path: Path) -> dict | None:
   133	    if not control_path.exists():
   134	        return None
   135	    try:
   136	        data = json.loads(control_path.read_text())
   137	    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   138	        return None
   139	    return data
   140	
   141	
   142	def main():
   143	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
   144	    ap.add_argument("--repo", required=True, help="scratch git repo to run aider in")
   145	    ap.add_argument("--variations", required=True, help="path to variations.yaml")
   146	    ap.add_argument("--log", default="error_log.jsonl")
   147	    ap.add_argument("--control", default="control.json")
   148	    ap.add_argument("--lmstudio-url", default="http://localhost:1234/v1")
   149	    ap.add_argument("--lmstudio-model", required=True,
   150	                     help="exact model id as shown in LM Studio's /v1/models")
   151	    ap.add_argument("--minutes", type=float, default=180)
   152	    ap.add_argument("--per-variation-timeout", type=int, default=None,
   153	                     help="overrides per_variation_timeout_seconds in the yaml")
   154	    args = ap.parse_args()
   155	
   156	    grid = yaml.safe_load(Path(args.variations).read_text())
   157	    combos = build_variations(grid)
   158	    timeout = args.per_variation_timeout or grid.get("per_variation_timeout_seconds", 180)
   159	
   160	    log_path = Path(args.log)
   161	    control_path = Path(args.control)
   162	    deadline = time.time() + args.minutes * 60
   163	
   164	    print(f"[run_variations] {len(combos)} variations queued, "
   165	          f"deadline in {args.minutes} min, logging to {log_path}")
   166	
   167	    for i, variation in enumerate(itertools.cycle(combos)):
   168	        if time.time() > deadline:
   169	            print("[run_variations] time budget exhausted, stopping.")
   170	            break
   171	
   172	        control = check_control(control_path)
   173	        if control and control.get("action") == "abort":
   174	            print(f"[run_variations] abort received: {control.get('reason', '')}")
   175	            break
   176	
   177	        result = run_aider(args.repo, grid["model"], variation, grid["message"], timeout)
   178	
   179	        if result["timed_out"]:
   180	            classification = {
   181	                "status": "fail",
   182	                "severity": "high",
   183	                "category": "timeout",
   184	                "likely_cause": f"Aider did not finish within {timeout}s",
   185	            }
   186	        else:
   187	            prompt = CLASSIFY_PROMPT.format(
   188	                command=result["command"],
   189	                exit_code=result["exit_code"],
   190	                stdout=result["stdout"][-1500:],
   191	                stderr=result["stderr"][-1500:],
   192	            )
   193	            classification = ask_gemma(args.lmstudio_url, args.lmstudio_model, prompt)
   194	
   195	        record = {
   196	            "iteration": i,
   197	            "timestamp": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S"),
   198	            "variation": variation,
   199	            **result,
   200	            "classification": classification,
   201	        }
   202	        with log_path.open("a") as f:
   203	            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")
   204	
   205	        print(f"[{i}] {variation} -> {classification.get('status')}/"
   206	              f"{classification.get('severity')} ({classification.get('category')})")
   207	
   208	        if i >= len(combos) * 20:  # sane upper bound even if minutes is huge
   209	            print("[run_variations] hit iteration safety cap, stopping.")
   210	            break
   211	
   212	    print("[run_variations] done.")
   213	
   214	
   215	if __name__ == "__main__":
   216	    sys.exit(main())

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""
     3	Frontier-model (Claude) check-in script. Run this every ~5 minutes while
     4	run_variations.py is running in the background.
     5	
     6	Usage:
     7	  python3 checkin.py --tail 20                  # just print a summary
     8	  python3 checkin.py --abort "reason text"       # summary + write abort to control.json
     9	  python3 checkin.py --continue                  # explicitly clear any prior abort
    10	"""
    11	import argparse
    12	import json
    13	from collections import Counter
    14	from pathlib import Path
    15	
    16	
    17	def load_tail(log_path: Path, n: int) -> list[dict]:
    18	    if not log_path.exists():
    19	        return []
    20	    lines = log_path.read_text().splitlines()[-n:]
    21	    records = []
    22	    for line in lines:
    23	        try:
    24	            records.append(json.loads(line))
    25	        except json.JSONDecodeError:
    26	            continue
    27	    return records
    28	
    29	
    30	def summarize(records: list[dict]) -> dict:
    31	    statuses = Counter(r.get("classification", {}).get("status") for r in records)
    32	    severities = Counter(r.get("classification", {}).get("severity") for r in records)
    33	    causes = Counter(r.get("classification", {}).get("likely_cause") for r in records)
    34	
    35	    # drift signal: same likely_cause 3+ times in a row at the tail
    36	    drift = False
    37	    if len(records) >= 3:
    38	        last_causes = [r.get("classification", {}).get("likely_cause") for r in records[-3:]]
    39	        drift = len(set(last_causes)) == 1 and last_causes[0] is not None
    40	
    41	    # suspicious pass: marked pass but nonzero exit code or traceback-like stderr
    42	    suspicious = [
    43	        r for r in records
    44	        if r.get("classification", {}).get("status") == "pass"
    45	        and (r.get("exit_code") not in (0, None) or "Traceback" in (r.get("stderr") or ""))
    46	    ]
    47	
    48	    return {
    49	        "n_records": len(records),
    50	        "statuses": dict(statuses),
    51	        "severities": dict(severities),
    52	        "top_causes": causes.most_common(5),
    53	        "drift_detected": drift,
    54	        "suspicious_pass_count": len(suspicious),
    55	    }
    56	
    57	
    58	def main():
    59	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    60	    ap.add_argument("--log", default="error_log.jsonl")
    61	    ap.add_argument("--control", default="control.json")
    62	    ap.add_argument("--tail", type=int, default=20, help="how many recent records to summarize")
    63	    ap.add_argument("--abort", metavar="REASON", help="write an abort signal to control.json")
    64	    ap.add_argument("--continue", dest="cont", action="store_true",
    65	                     help="clear control.json / explicitly signal continue")
    66	    args = ap.parse_args()
    67	
    68	    log_path = Path(args.log)
    69	    control_path = Path(args.control)
    70	
    71	    records = load_tail(log_path, args.tail)
    72	    summary = summarize(records)
    73	
    74	    print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
    75	
    76	    if summary["drift_detected"]:
    77	        print("\n⚠ DRIFT: last 3 records share the same likely_cause — "
    78	              "worker may be stuck. Consider --abort.")
    79	    if summary["suspicious_pass_count"] > 0:
    80	        print(f"\n⚠ {summary['suspicious_pass_count']} record(s) marked 'pass' but show "
    81	              "a nonzero exit code or traceback — classification may be unreliable.")
    82	
    83	    if args.abort:
    84	        control_path.write_text(json.dumps({"action": "abort", "reason": args.abort}))
    85	        print(f"\n[checkin] wrote abort to {control_path}: {args.abort}")
    86	    elif args.cont:
    87	        control_path.write_text(json.dumps({"action": "continue"}))
    88	        print(f"\n[checkin] wrote continue to {control_path}")
    89	
    90	
    91	if __name__ == "__main__":
    92	    main()

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""
     3	Compile error_log.jsonl into one GitHub issue, triaged by severity.
     4	
     5	Requires the `gh` CLI, already authenticated (`gh auth status`).
     6	
     7	Usage:
     8	  python3 compile_issue.py --log error_log.jsonl --repo owner/repo
     9	  python3 compile_issue.py --log error_log.jsonl --repo owner/repo --dry-run
    10	"""
    11	import argparse
    12	import json
    13	import subprocess
    14	from collections import defaultdict
    15	from pathlib import Path
    16	
    17	SEVERITY_ORDER = ["critical", "high", "medium", "low", "none", "unknown"]
    18	
    19	
    20	def load_records(log_path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    21	    records = []
    22	    for line in log_path.read_text().splitlines():
    23	        line = line.strip()
    24	        if not line:
    25	            continue
    26	        try:
    27	            records.append(json.loads(line))
    28	        except json.JSONDecodeError:
    29	            continue
    30	    return records
    31	
    32	
    33	def group_by_severity(records: list[dict]) -> dict:
    34	    groups = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
    35	    for r in records:
    36	        c = r.get("classification", {})
    37	        if c.get("status") == "pass" and c.get("severity") in (None, "none"):
    38	            continue  # clean pass, no need to report
    39	        sev = c.get("severity", "unknown")
    40	        # signature = category + first ~60 chars of likely_cause, so near-duplicate
    41	        # failures collapse into one bucket instead of one row per iteration
    42	        sig = f"{c.get('category', 'uncategorized')} :: {(c.get('likely_cause') or '')[:60]}"
    43	        groups[sev][sig].append(r)
    44	    return groups
    45	
    46	
    47	def build_body(groups: dict, total: int) -> str:
    48	    lines = [
    49	        "Automated rollup from a Gemma-driven Aider variation test run.",
    50	        f"Total variations logged: {total}.",
    51	        "",
    52	    ]
    53	    for sev in SEVERITY_ORDER:
    54	        buckets = groups.get(sev)
    55	        if not buckets:
    56	            continue
    57	        lines.append(f"## {sev.upper()} ({sum(len(v) for v in buckets.values())} instances)")
    58	        lines.append("")
    59	        for sig, instances in sorted(buckets.items(), key=lambda kv: -len(kv[1])):
    60	            example = instances[0]
    61	            lines.append(f"- [ ] **{sig}** — seen {len(instances)}x")
    62	            lines.append(f"  - example command: `{example.get('command', '')}`")
    63	            lines.append(f"  - example variation: `{json.dumps(example.get('variation', {}))}`")
    64	            if example.get("stderr"):
    65	                snippet = example["stderr"].strip().splitlines()[-1][:200]
    66	                lines.append(f"  - stderr tail: `{snippet}`")
    67	        lines.append("")
    68	    return "\n".join(lines)
    69	
    70	
    71	def main():
    72	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    73	    ap.add_argument("--log", required=True)
    74	    ap.add_argument("--repo", required=True, help="owner/repo for `gh issue create --repo`")
    75	    ap.add_argument("--title", default="Aider -> OpenRouter -> GLM 5.2 pipeline: variation test triage")
    76	    ap.add_argument("--label", action="append", default=None,
    77	                     help="repeatable; defaults to bug,aider-pipeline if omitted")
    78	    ap.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="print the issue body, don't call gh")
    79	    args = ap.parse_args()
    80	    labels = args.label or ["bug", "aider-pipeline"]
    81	
    82	    records = load_records(Path(args.log))
    83	    if not records:
    84	        print("No records found in log — nothing to file.")
    85	        return
    86	
    87	    groups = group_by_severity(records)
    88	    body = build_body(groups, len(records))
    89	
    90	    if args.dry_run:
    91	        print(body)
    92	        return
    93	
    94	    body_path = Path("issue_body.md")
    95	    body_path.write_text(body)
    96	
    97	    cmd = [
    98	        "gh", "issue", "create",
    99	        "--repo", args.repo,
   100	        "--title", args.title,
   101	        "--body-file", str(body_path),
   102	    ]
   103	    for label in labels:
   104	        cmd += ["--label", label]
   105	
   106	    result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
   107	    if result.returncode == 0:
   108	        print(f"Issue created: {result.stdout.strip()}")
   109	    else:
   110	        print(f"gh issue create failed:\n{result.stderr}")
   111	        print(f"Issue body was written to {body_path} — you can file it manually.")
   112	
   113	
   114	if __name__ == "__main__":
   115	    main()

./CHANGELOG.md:19:- **[GH-189](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/189) — PDDA's issue↔doc sync has a blind spot created by its own recommended remediation.** `check_issue_doc_sync()` (`utils/pdda/pdda.sh:507-542`) scans only `pdda_list_working_docs()`, scoped to `2-WORKING` (`pdda-lib.sh:178`). Its direction (a) fires on *issue CLOSED + doc still in `2-WORKING`* and prints a `git mv … 3-COMPLETED` remediation — and **performing that remediation removes the doc from the check's scan set permanently.** `PDDA_COMPLETED_DIR` (`pdda-lib.sh:10`) is referenced *only* to construct that recommendation string; no check ever reads a doc out of `3-COMPLETED`. Direction (b) is likewise `2-WORKING`-only, and nothing at all validates a `ROADMAP.md` ledger entry's status marker against issue state — `check_roadmap()` (`pdda.sh:227-267`) lints structure only (task-list items, execution-detail headings, line/heading caps). Net: the sweep half of the workflow is enforced, the ROADMAP/CHANGELOG flip half is not, and completing the enforced half silences the only watcher. Demonstrated by the Marathon Plan C drift above — 8 CLOSED issues, a `3-COMPLETED` doc still reading `status: captured`, a ROADMAP cell reading "Ready to fire", and `pdda.sh run` reporting "all checks passed" for four days. It nearly caused a duplicate rebuild of the risk-4 containment kernel (`relay-turn-lib.sh`). Proposed fix (both warn-only, both degrading to the existing offline INFO path): a new `pdda-check-roadmap-issue-state` (CLOSED issue + non-terminal ledger marker → warn, and the inverse), plus extending `check_issue_doc_sync` to flag a `3-COMPLETED` doc whose frontmatter `status` lead word is non-terminal. Capture doc: [GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md). *(PDDA is not a standalone repo — it lives at `utils/pdda/` here, so the issue and ledger entry both land in `xyz-3-agents-swarm`.)*
./CHANGELOG.md:43:Triage-only pass, no fixes landed yet. During the GH-158/161/162/164 housekeeping pass, re-confirmed (again, via `git stash`) that `validate.sh`'s 9 failures are pre-existing and unrelated to that day's merges — same conclusion already reached once during the GH-165 review via a parent-commit worktree comparison. Rather than let them stay untracked debt, triaged each to a concrete failure signature + fix direction: `analyze.sh` and `relay-token-collision.sh` are confirmed flaky (state-leak family, same class as GH-133 though different mechanisms — SIGPIPE vs. fixture task-name collision); the other 7 (`cost.sh`, `watchdog-relay.sh`, `deep-research.sh`, `new-relay.sh`, `find-harness.sh`, `transcript-audit.sh`, `marathon-plan.sh`) look like independent deterministic bugs, each isolated to one file. Filed as a single umbrella issue ([GH-170-VALIDATE-FAILING-TESTS.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-170-VALIDATE-FAILING-TESTS.md)) rather than 9 separate issues, then queued as [Marathon Plan F](PROJECT/2-WORKING/MARATHON-PLAN-2026-07-07-F-VALIDATE-FIXES.md) with 9 independent, parallel-safe lanes (one per test file, no shared write-set). Not yet fired.
./CHANGELOG.md:52:Built GH-161's Phase 1 shape (explored earlier the same day; [PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-161-HARNESS-OBSERVABILITY.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-161-HARNESS-OBSERVABILITY.md)): `rtl_trace()` (opt-in via `RTL_TRACE=1`) and `rtl_log_always()` (mirrors an existing unconditional diagnostic, no new gating) in [relay-automation/relay-turn-lib.sh](relay-automation/relay-turn-lib.sh), instrumenting root resolution (`rtl_init`), allowlist match/reject (`rtl_check`), worktree seed/copy-back (`rtl_worktree_begin`/`rtl_worktree_end`), and the containment/token-handoff branches (`rtl_enforce`) — all written into the turn's own transcript (`CODEX_LOG`/`AGY_LOG`), never a new log file. Also closed GH-165's actual root gap: `CODEX_LOG`/`AGY_LOG` now default to a persistent path via the existing `rtl_transcript_root` resolver (`relay-system/logs/<date>/`, gitignored) instead of a PID-keyed tmp file that vanished with the process. `RTL_LOG` is exported in both `codex-turn.sh`/`agy-turn.sh` **before** `rtl_init` runs, and the agent-binary redirect switched from truncating (`>`) to appending (`>>`) so it no longer wipes the trace line `rtl_init` already wrote. The new end-to-end test (`test/relay-turn-trace.sh`) caught two real bugs before they shipped: a `set -e`-under-`test && action` idiom that silently killed an otherwise-successful turn whenever nothing needed sweeping, and the truncating-redirect data loss above — both fixed. Verification: `test/relay-turn-trace.sh` **20/20** (new, added to `validate.sh`); the full existing containment/shim regression set (`codex-turn.sh`, `agy-turn.sh`, `worktree-isolation.sh`, `shim-worktree.sh`, `claude-turn.sh`, `aider-turn.sh`, `archive-commit.sh`, `relay-artifact-file.sh`, `relay-dep-drift.sh`, `relay-turn-handoff.sh`, `relay-turn-timeout.sh`) all still green; `skills/relay-automation/relay-pkg.tar.gz` regenerated. `./validate.sh`'s remaining 4 failures (`archive-writers.sh`, `xyz-harness-hooks.sh`, `security-scan.sh`, `python:test_python_layer.py`) were independently reproduced against a clean `origin/main` baseline via `git stash` — pre-existing, unrelated to this change.
./CHANGELOG.md:71:Per operator direction ("drive all three to full close"), opened a marathon bundling #142/#143/#144 ([PROJECT/2-WORKING/MARATHON-142-143-144.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/MARATHON-142-143-144.md)): #142 as an Opus-direct code lane, #143/#144 as parallel Sonnet doc lanes on disjoint write-sets. **#142 (agy reliability) CLOSED.** Verification found Phase 2 was *already shipped* (`dcc1505`) but the doc never caught up: both `agy-turn` shims (`relay-automation/agy-turn.sh` + the `XYZ_PYTHON` port `utils/py/agy-turn.py`, in parity) pre-validate `AGY_MODEL` against `agy models` and **fail loudly (exit 5)** instead of silently degrading to the default — the one *confirmed* live failure from Phase 1 (S9/F8). Regression-locked by `test/agy-turn.sh` cases 7-8, wired into `validate.sh:39` (33/0 green). Recorded the **Phase-3 recommendation: KEEP-GATED** — agy is trustworthy for reviewer turns (clean contained dogfood 2026-06-21) but producer turns should run behind `RELAY_WORKTREE_ISOLATION=1` until the remaining characterized gaps close; the one confirmed-unsafe behavior is fixed. Deferred gaps (S2 role-adherence, S5 distraction, S8 cost-blind, controlled S10 repro) — explicitly "separate later slices" per the Phase-2 contract — spun into follow-up **[#155](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/155)**. Doc → `3-COMPLETED`.
./CHANGELOG.md:83:Reworked [README.md](README.md) to lead with a **Beta Tester Onboarding** section, mirroring the refined onboarding posted as the third message on [issue #123](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/123#issuecomment-4895454902). The section covers the four modes of operation (Consult → Relay → Swarm → Marathon, framed as a trust ladder), branch/worktree safety with an explicit per-step reversibility map (skill install / relay / consult / PDDA install and how to undo each), and the key message that **Relay and Consult deliver immediate value with no PDDA** — PDDA is only for the full automation path (Swarm/Marathon). A banner marks the section as temporary: once the beta wraps, it moves out and the normal README resumes at "What XYZ is" (original intro preserved under that new heading). Alignment sweep of the rest of the README against the tree: all referenced files verified present; one stale link fixed (`AUTOMATED-RELAY.md` had moved `PROJECT/2-WORKING/` → `PROJECT/4-MISC/`); glossary now cross-links the four-modes section.
./CHANGELOG.md:152:A headless **Codex** cross-model review of the shipped HQ feature (relay thread committed on `main`, `4158bd0`; verdict *Changes requested*) surfaced four untested edge cases, now fixed on `worktree-hq-gh132-hardening` → PR. **Blocker 1** — basename-only resolution could pick the wrong repo; `hq_xyz_lookup` now preserves `owner/repo` identity, disambiguates an install collision by each candidate's real git origin slug, and returns **AMBIGUOUS** (`rc=2`) rather than guessing. **Blocker 2** — a stale XYZ path (registry row pointing at a deleted dir) could make `fire` print `GATES PASS` at a dead target; stale rows are now skipped (`[ -d ]`), so they can't count as an install or arm Tier A. **Should 3** — `park --title` wrote unescaped YAML and still exited 0; added `hq_yaml_dq` escaping and made `park --create` **fail hard** when the frontmatter check fails. **Should 4** — a glob token (`resolve '*'`) matched via the `find` fallback; `hq_fs_find` now refuses glob-metachar tokens. New `test/hq-hardening.sh` **11/11** (each fix reproduces the old failure first), in `validate.sh`; full HQ suite 90 checks green; shellcheck (`-S warning`) + `pdda.sh run` clean. Issue [#132].
./CHANGELOG.md:168:- **Fuzzy resolution (Phase 1.x)** — `hq_norm` (lowercase + alnum-only) + `hq_candidates` add a fallback when exact resolution misses: "rebalanceOS" now resolves to `rebalance-OS`; a genuinely ambiguous name (e.g. "acme" → acme-app + acme-api) returns rc=2 with `CANDIDATES` rather than guessing. `hq_resolve` now emits `RESOLVED_VIA` (exact|fuzzy); status/park surface the fuzzy note + ambiguous candidates.
./CHANGELOG.md:290:Phase 2 redirected the transcript *writers*; the containment kernel still committed the relay file into the target repo. Phase 3 closes that: when `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` places the relay file in a **separate archive git repo**, the kernel commits the **transcript into the archive** while the **code artifact + the `.tick` token stay anchored to the target**. Built and reviewed operator-direct under Opus 4.8 (kernel lane — not headless).
./CHANGELOG.md:303:Phase 1 built the resolver but wired it to nothing. Phase 2 makes each writer call it, so setting `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` actually redirects transcripts out of a foreign product repo. Done in an isolated git worktree while another agent worked `main`.
./CHANGELOG.md:306:- **Scope boundary (honest):** Phase 2 redirects the transcript *writers*. A cross-repo relay *turn* is not fully redirected until **Phase 3** — the turn's own relay-file writes are still governed by the target-repo write-allowlist in `relay-turn-lib.sh` (containment + commit-into-archive is the risk-4 core). `consult.sh` / `swarm-preflight.sh` (no containment) are fully redirected today.
./CHANGELOG.md:311:The relay/consult/marathon/swarm tooling writes its operational transcript *inside* the target repo (`$ROOT/relay-system/<date>/…`), so running against a foreign product repo B pollutes B with a `relay-system/` tree and lands generic relay commits in B's history. GH-30 adds one optional env var to redirect that; this is **Phase 1 — the safe standalone slice: the resolver only, no writer wiring (Phase 2) and no containment/commit changes (the risk-4 Phase 3).**
./CHANGELOG.md:365:- **Fix** — the shim redirects Aider's chat/input/llm history to a throwaway dir outside the repo via `--chat-history-file`/`--input-history-file`/`--llm-history-file` (`AIDER_AUX_DIR`, default `$TMPDIR/aider-aux-$$`). Re-run confirmed **exit 0**, a file-scoped commit (relay file only), **no `.aider.*` leak**, token handed to the peer.
./CHANGELOG.md:366:- **Regression** — the `test/aider-turn.sh` stub now mimics Aider's aux-file write (honors `--chat-history-file`, defaults to CWD), so dropping the redirect flips the good turn to exit 6. New no-leak assertion; **32/32** (verified by a strip-and-restore teeth-check). Doc moved earlier to `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED`; the "live E2E" follow-on is now done.
./CHANGELOG.md:619:- **Independent QA (automated agy `/relay-xyz`, 2 rounds → Approved):** an agy review of `install.sh` found 3 real hardening gaps — a `cd "-"` sentinel corruption (reachable only via explicit `--repo -`/`TICK_REPO_ROOT=-`; default path was already safe, severity corrected Blocker→Should), a non-fail-open registry-init redirection (unwritable `~/.config/xyz` could abort under `set -e`), and a temp-file leak on `mv` failure. All fixed in `install.sh` **and** both SKILL copies (lockstep), re-verified adversarially (`--repo -` → clean 5-field row; unwritable registry → exit 0 fail-open; idempotent), `./validate.sh` still **69/69**. agy re-reviewed the live files (behaviorally proven, ran the installer) → **Approved**. Thread: [gh62-install-registry-qa.md](relay-system/2026-06-30/gh62-install-registry-qa.md). Containment held both rounds (an off-lane `failing.sh` agy created to probe `set -e` was reverted; its `temp/` test files stayed in the isolated worktree).

./CHANGELOG.md:704:- **Verification:** `verify-fixture.sh` folds mutated vs control: mutated → `claimed 0` (silent resurrection), control (no injected event) → `done 0`. A **blind** Reviewer agent (only `CANDIDATE.md`; `EXPECTED.md`/`README.md`/`verify-fixture.sh` held out) found the fault, **ran its own control experiment**, traced *why* the fence misses it in `src/project.js`, confirmed status=`claimed`/rejections=0, and proposed the systemic fix (terminality dominates the epoch fence; reject post-terminal claims into the log; reopen must be an explicit `task.reopened` event). Gate **passed**.

./CHANGELOG.md:1174:- **Kernel fence (`src/project.js` `fold` → `foldWithMeta`):** the current owner is the live claim with the **highest epoch**; a mutating event (`done`/`circuit_break`/`scope_changed`/`released`) is honoured **only** from that owner at `epoch >= owner.epoch`, else rejected and never applied. Two load-bearing sub-invariants: (1) a `released` retires a claim only if `release.epoch >= claim.epoch` — without it a replayed lower-epoch release would retire the current same-id claim (the keystone failure); (2) a handoff (`release --to`) is honoured only from the latest epoch, so a displaced writer can't redirect the reservation.

Coordination is **local-transport** (since Run 2): every verb is a pure append to a shared local `.tick/events/` — no git push or fetch per event. Peer agents see each other's events by reading the same shared directory, and a per-repo `O_EXCL` lock (`withClaimLock`, under `.tick/locks/`) serialises concurrent claims into a real mutex. `git` is used only incidentally — `tick` locates the repo root via `git rev-parse`, and `tick reap` defaults its actor name from `git config user.name`. The hardest one is source-of-truth: it says reads are canonical from `.tick`/`STATE.md`, then later says agent work is handed off from `PROJECT/**` “alone,” which cannot both be true as written.
./relay-system/2026-07-02/gp-contradictions-124824/gp-contradictions.codex.md:233:“`Reads go through the projection`” and “`Nothing canonical lives in two places where it can drift`” ([GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:24](</private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-88217-2272/GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:24>)) conflict with “`Agent work is stoppable, resumable, and handed off from PROJECT/** alone`” ([GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:38](</private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-88217-2272/GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:38>)). The `Fresh` bar sharpens the conflict: “`A turn that reads a stale STATE.md ... is wrong by construction`” ([GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:15](</private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-88217-2272/GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:15>)). If fresh runtime state must come from the projection, then `PROJECT/**` cannot be sufficient “alone”; if `PROJECT/**` is sufficient alone, Principle 2 is overstated.

./PROJECT/1-INBOX/FEEDBACK/agy-1.0.10-hang-bug-report.md:73:When `relay-xyz` is requested in a repo that lacks `relay-automation/`, there is no harness to run and no graceful, operator-visible fallback — the driver must hand-roll the entire loop (prompt assembly, subprocess invocation, output capture, block append, header flip, commit). The skill description says it's "NOT for repos without relay-automation/," but in practice the operator's "do a relay-xyz with agy" request just quietly becomes a manual improvisation. A detect-and-redirect ("no relay-automation/ here → use portable /relay") would make the degradation explicit.

./relay-system/2026-07-02/gh45-review-183600/gh45-review.agy.md:33:* **Finding:** Keys are anchored to `${TICK_REPO_ROOT:-$ROOT_DIR}` / `${TICK_REPO_ROOT:-$ROOT}`. Test suites redirect this to a temp directory, keeping host runs hermetic.
./decisions/2026-06-18-epoch-fencing.md:12:**Decision:** Add a monotonic, per-task `epoch` to the `tick` event schema (bump `schema_version` 0.1.0 → 0.2.0) and make the projection kernel (`src/project.js` `fold`) **fence** any mutating event whose epoch is below the current owner's. A displaced writer — even one that revives with the **same agent id** — can no longer advance (`done`/`circuit_break`), corrupt (`scope_changed`), retire (`released`), or redirect the handoff of a task it no longer owns. Rejected events are recorded in a deterministic audit log (`.tick/rejected.jsonl`, surfaced by `tick fences`).

./PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-77-AIDER-OPENROUTER-LANE.md:31:| **✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-02 — marathon + relay + consult.** New `relay-automation/aider-turn.sh` headless turn-taker drives Aider against OpenRouter (OpenAI-standard) behind the shared `relay-turn-lib.sh` containment core — **discrete from Codex** (no shared code/env). Two Aider-specific adaptations: the SHIM performs the tick token ops (`claim <task> --paths` + `ping`; `rtl_enforce` GH-67 does the release/done) since Aider can't run shell mid-turn — and **asserts ownership (`claimer == self` via `tick info`) before launching Aider, failing the turn (exit 5) if the claim didn't stick** so a turn can never commit with the token still open under the old owner, and it runs Aider with `--no-auto-commits` so the harness owns the commit (else the commit-bypass guard trips). Additive routing in `marathon-agent.sh` + `marathon-drive.sh` (`aider*` builder lane; Codex paths byte-identical) — the same dispatcher `relay-drive.sh` uses, so Aider is a first-class **relay** turn-taker too. **Also wired into `consult.sh`** (`--models …,aider`) — the cross-model advisor behind `relay-drive.sh --consult-verify`. `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` pre-flight fails fast. `test/aider-turn.sh` (32 checks, incl. relay-dispatch + token-ownership guard + aux-file redirect) + `test/consult.sh` (+2 aider checks) in `validate.sh` — green. **Live E2E DONE 2026-07-03** — a real `gpt-4o-mini` turn via OpenRouter, which surfaced + fixed the aux-file containment bug (below). | Optional follow-on: add `aider-turn.sh` to the vendored `relay-pkg` (make-pkg) if a consumer needs it. |
./PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-77-AIDER-OPENROUTER-LANE.md:33:> **Live-E2E fix (2026-07-03).** The first real Aider turn failed **exit 6** — Aider writes its own `.aider.chat.history.md` + `.aider.input.history` into CWD, which (with `--no-gitignore`) land untracked and trip `rtl_enforce`'s off-allowlist guard, so **every real turn failed** even though the stub tests (which never create them) passed. Fix: the shim redirects Aider's chat/input/llm history files to a throwaway dir outside the repo via `--chat-history-file`/`--input-history-file`/`--llm-history-file`. Re-run confirmed **exit 0**, a file-scoped commit (relay file only), no `.aider.*` leak, token handed to the peer. Regression: the test stub now mimics Aider's aux-file write, so dropping the redirect flips the good turn back to exit 6.

./ROADMAP.md:82:- **GH-189 · PDDA: sweeping a doc to `3-COMPLETED` silences the only issue-state check watching it — shipped items can sit in ROADMAP as "ready to fire" indefinitely** 🆕 **captured 2026-07-08 · rated · self-inflicted blind spot, found by tripping over it** — `check_issue_doc_sync()` (`pdda.sh:507-542`) scans only `pdda_list_working_docs()` (`2-WORKING`, `pdda-lib.sh:178`); its direction (a) recommends `git mv`-ing a CLOSED-issue doc into `3-COMPLETED`, and **performing that remediation removes the doc from the check's scan set permanently** (`PDDA_COMPLETED_DIR` is referenced only to build the recommendation string — no check ever reads a doc out of it). Nothing validates a ROADMAP entry's status marker against issue state either; `check_roadmap()` lints structure only. Observed: Marathon Plan C shipped 2026-07-04, but ROADMAP advertised its Wave 1 + `#107` kernel track as "Ready to fire" for four days with all 8 issues CLOSED, while `pdda.sh run` reported "all checks passed" throughout — nearly causing a duplicate rebuild of the risk-4 containment kernel. Fix: a `pdda-check-roadmap-issue-state` check (CLOSED issue + non-terminal ledger marker → warn, and the inverse), plus extending `check_issue_doc_sync` to flag a `3-COMPLETED` doc whose frontmatter `status` lead word is non-terminal. Both warn-only, both offline-degrading. cx/risk/eff 2/2/2. → [GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md) · [#189](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/189)
./ROADMAP.md:88:- **GH-159 · hq-lib.sh: `hq_repo_resolve` reports ambiguous when the same path appears twice as a candidate** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-07-08 (PR #179, `39729a0`); closed 2026-07-08** — `hq.sh resolve sleuth-app` returned `CANDIDATES=<path>,<path>` (the identical path twice, from two discovery sources) and bailed out `RESOLVED_VIA=ambiguous` / empty `REPO_PATH` instead of deduping to the one real match. Fixed in `hq_xyz_lookup` (`utils/hq/hq-lib.sh`) via a `seen_coords` dedup before the `n>1` collision check, confirmed live in `main`. cx/risk/eff 2/1/2. → [GH-159-HQ-REPO-RESOLVE-DEDUPE.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-159-HQ-REPO-RESOLVE-DEDUPE.md) · [#159](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/159)
./ROADMAP.md:98:- **GH-127 · swarm-preflight: GH-54 fs-touching test detector misses bare `>` redirects** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-07-04 (`691848c`, bundled into the [#108 lane](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-108-SWARM-PREFLIGHT-HARDENING-BUNDLE.md) — same file)** — the fs-touching regex caught `cat >`, `printf...>`, `>>`, `mktemp`, etc., but not a bare single `>` (e.g. `echo x > file`), a common and equally filesystem-touching pattern; a test using only that form got the weaker self-verify guidance instead of GH-54's "do not run in-turn" rule. Fixed by bare-`>` detection. Found reviewing PR #125. → [#127](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/127)
./ROADMAP.md:101:- **GH-108 · `--pre-advance-cmd 'npm test -- <name>'` runs the whole suite, unrelated flakes fail a green lane** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-07-04 (`691848c`, 85/85) — bundle also closed #126/#127 (same file) · part of [Marathon Plan C](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/MARATHON-PLAN-2026-07-04-C-RELIABILITY.md)** — `swarm-preflight.sh` passed a contract's `gate` field verbatim as `--pre-advance-cmd`; whether `npm test -- <name>` actually filters depends on the target repo's own test script, and often doesn't (reproduced live: an unrelated `EADDRINUSE` flake failed an otherwise 67/67-green lane). Fixed (Level 1, documented-caveat): when `GATE_CMD` heuristically looks like a filtered-runner invocation, the packet emits an explicit scoping caveat rather than attempting cross-runner auto-detection. Landed in one lane with **#126** (GH-55 covering-test substring-match tightening) and **#127** (GH-54 bare-`>` fs-touching gap) since all three touch `utils/swarm-preflight.sh`. cx/risk/eff 3/2/3. → [GH-108-SWARM-PREFLIGHT-HARDENING-BUNDLE.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-108-SWARM-PREFLIGHT-HARDENING-BUNDLE.md) · [#108](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/108) · [#126](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/126) · [#127](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/127)

Delete the shim, remove its README row and `GEMINI_AGENT` comment from `marathon-agent.sh`. `agy-turn.sh` is the live permanent replacement. **✅ Follow-up gap found + fixed 2026-07-03:** the original `2b5f8a3` fix deleted `relay-automation/gemini-turn.sh` and dropped `gemini` from `marathon-drive.sh`'s help/validation, but left 5 test files still hardcoding `--reviewer gemini` / `RELAY_AGENT=gemini` / a direct reference to the deleted shim (`test/gemini-turn.sh`, `test/relay-turn-timeout.sh`, `test/marathon-yaml.sh`, `test/marathon-drive.sh`, `test/xyz-harness-hooks.sh`) — surfaced when today's GH-118/119/120 marathon ran the full suite (6 failures, all traced to this one root cause via zero-diff confirmation against pre-marathon HEAD).
