./ROADMAP.md:117:- **GH-30 · optional centralized transcript archive** ✅ **ALL PHASES SHIPPED 2026-07-03 (Plan A lane 5 complete)** — optional `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` redirects relay/consult/marathon transcripts out of each foreign repo's `relay-system/` into one namespaced archive; default unset = byte-for-byte current. **Phases 1–2:** single resolver `rtl_transcript_root` + `rtl_repo_slug` in `relay-turn-lib.sh` (Model-A validation: absolute + exists + git repo, else HARD ERROR), and all writers derive their transcript base from it. **Phase 3 (Model A, risk-4 core):** `rtl_init` flags `RTL_ARCHIVE_MODE` when the relay file's git repo ≠ `RTL_ROOT`, and `rtl_enforce` commits the **transcript into the archive** via an isolated `git -C` pathspec commit while the **code artifact + `.tick` token stay on the target** — target tree free of `relay-system/`, no transcript commit in target history, and the isolated archive commit can never orphan a concurrent peer commit (GH-13 guard target-only, holds when token-tree ≠ transcript-tree). **Phase 4:** `extract-relay-telemetry.sh` reads the resolver, aggregating across all `<repo-slug>/` dirs. **Phase 5:** `new-relay.sh` wired; `CONSUMING.md` + `README.md` document the contract; CHANGELOG bet recorded. New `test/archive-commit.sh` (16) + `test/archive-telemetry.sh` (3) → `validate.sh` green. cx/risk/eff 3/4/3. Kernel lane (`relay-turn-lib.sh`). → [GH-30-CENTRALIZED-TRANSCRIPT-ARCHIVE.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-30-CENTRALIZED-TRANSCRIPT-ARCHIVE.md) · [#30](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/30)
./ROADMAP.md:120:- **Dueling/relay · commit-signal advance (file-driven mode)** 🟡 — **built + tested 2026-06-26** (agy review pending): `poll.sh --turn-source file` reads whose-turn from the relay `NEXT:` field, making the tick token **optional** — exactly what the first cross-repo dueling run needed when the Maintainer Claude worked straight off the relay file and never joined `tick`. Optional `--peer-commit-repo`/`--peer-commit-match` add the "advance on the peer's fix commit" signal (idle → run-runner only once a matching commit lands). `relay_field` now tolerates `**bold**` markdown keys; STATUS terminal + artifact-scope-clean still apply. 7 new assertions in [test/poll-driver.sh](test/poll-driver.sh); `validate.sh` **48/48**. → [poll.sh](relay-automation/poll.sh) · [Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./ROADMAP.md:121:- **Dueling/relay · token resilience for a non-participating peer + multi-round reuse** 🟡 — **core addressed by `--turn-source file`** (token-optional ⇒ no `parked suspect` stall and no `spent/reserved — not claimable` mid-run, the two lock failures from 2026-06-26). Residual (only when you DO use the tick token): a non-watchdog `reap`+`claim` reconcile path (still manual) and token-reuse semantics that survive N `release --to peer` round-trips. Lower priority now that file-source is the recommended dueling path. → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./ROADMAP.md:122:- **Gate design · convergence gates that pin a *worse* code shape** 🐞 — the KWFS `#70` gate statically extracts a literal `return array(...)` from *each* of three formatter methods and asserts equality, which makes the issue's intended fix (one canonical method + delegate/delete the duplicates) **fail the gate** — the Maintainer was forced to keep three duplicated-but-identical literals, divergence the gate calls "converged." Generalizes: static-extraction needle gates can pin a less-maintainable shape than the refactor they exist to enforce. Fix direction: assert runtime output-key equality (or canonical + delegation), not three literals. → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./ROADMAP.md:123:- **Orchestration · in-loop gate verification must run sandbox-off** 🟡 — an orchestrator/watcher re-running a target gate (`bash tests/run.sh`) under the Claude Bash sandbox **false-fails** (PHP can't create lock files → bogus "syntax errors"); the peer's un-sandboxed run is authoritative. Handle: in-loop gate self-checks run sandbox-off, or trust the peer's run + spot-confirm sandbox-off (memory: `codex-cli-needs-sandbox-disabled` family). → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/4-MISC/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./ROADMAP.md:149:- **GH-161 · Audit and add more observability into the harness and individual files** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-07-07 (branch `claude/gh-161-harness-observability`)** — added `rtl_trace()`/`rtl_log_always()`/`rtl_default_log()` to `relay-turn-lib.sh`, instrumenting root resolution, allowlist match/reject, worktree seed/copy-back, and every containment/token-handoff branch — written into the turn's own transcript (`CODEX_LOG`/`AGY_LOG` via `RTL_LOG`), not a new log file. Fine-grained tracing is opt-in (`RTL_TRACE=1`); the existing unconditional diagnostics now also land in the transcript unconditionally. Also fixed the actual GH-165 root gap: `CODEX_LOG`/`AGY_LOG` now default to a persistent path (`rtl_transcript_root(...)/logs/<date>/`, gitignored) instead of a PID-keyed tmp file. The build's own new test caught two real bugs first (a `set -e`-under-`test && action` footgun that silently killed successful turns, and a truncating-redirect that wiped `rtl_init`'s own trace line) — both fixed before landing. `test/relay-turn-trace.sh` 20/20 (new); full existing containment/shim regression set green; `validate.sh`'s 4 remaining failures independently reproduced against a clean `origin/main` baseline (pre-existing, unrelated). cx/risk/eff 3/1/3. → [GH-161-HARNESS-OBSERVABILITY.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-161-HARNESS-OBSERVABILITY.md) · [#161](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/161)

./ROADMAP.md:180:- **GH-64 · Security-scanning guardrail in the review chain** ✅ **Tool + test SHIPPED 2026-07-01 (`a0cc84e`, marathon Wave 2 Lane A); ACTIVE BLOCKING GATE wired 2026-07-01** — `relay-automation/hooks/security-scan.sh`: self-contained, no-network static scanner for shell scripts; fail-loud (`SECURITY: <file>:<line> [rule]` to stderr, non-zero exit), never auto-fixes. Rules: eval-of-variable, `curl`/`wget`-piped-to-shell, AWS keys, PEM private-key headers, GitHub PATs, Slack tokens, literal credential assignments. **Active-gate follow-on (this session):** a hand-maintained, checked-in baseline (`relay-automation/hooks/security-scan-baseline.txt`, exact `file\trule\tline-text` triples, matched by content not line number) suppresses the known-legitimate findings (reviewed dispatcher `eval`s in `poll.sh`/`relay-drive.sh`/`marathon-drive.sh`/`improve-loop.sh`, doc-comment prose, the scanner's own test fixtures) WITHOUT hiding them — every baselined finding still prints (labeled `SECURITY (baselined)`), per GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md #8; a genuinely new finding still fails loud. New `--no-baseline` (raw scan) and `--tsv` (machine-parseable rows for authoring baseline entries) flags. `test/security-scan.sh` now 28 checks including a real-repo-against-the-checked-in-baseline assertion — that assertion IS the blocking gate, already wired into `validate.sh` (no separate CI step needed). Confirmed NO real secret leak in any of the 45 baselined findings. → [GH-64-SECURITY-SCANNING-GUARDRAIL.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-64-SECURITY-SCANNING-GUARDRAIL.md) · [#64](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/64)

A cross-repo relay *turn* is not fully redirected until **Phase 3** (containment allowlist + commit-into-archive), because the turn's own relay-file writes are still governed by the target-repo write-allowlist in `relay-turn-lib.sh`. `consult.sh` and `swarm-preflight.sh` (no containment) are fully redirected today.
./PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-30-CENTRALIZED-TRANSCRIPT-ARCHIVE.md:144:- [x] Ensure the relay turn-token (`.tick/`) stays anchored to the **target** repo even when the transcript is redirected — the GH-67 handoff already uses `TICK_REPO_ROOT`; archive mode never touches it, so token-tree and transcript-tree differ safely.
./PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-30-CENTRALIZED-TRANSCRIPT-ARCHIVE.md:205:  "remediation": { "source": "GH-30#phase-0-model-A", "criteria": "A single resolver in relay-turn-lib.sh returns $XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT/relay-system/<repo-slug> when set (absolute + existing git repo, else HARD ERROR — never silent fallback) and $ROOT/relay-system when unset (byte-for-byte current path); all transcript writers (consult/marathon-drive/relay-drive/swarm-preflight/extract-relay-telemetry) call it; explicit per-call overrides (OUT=/--relay-file) still win. MODEL A: commit the transcript into the SEPARATE archive git repo via an isolated step that keeps TICK_REPO_ROOT + allowlist anchored on the TARGET repo and never orphans a peer commit in the target tree. NEW test/archive-root.sh covers unset / set / set-but-missing / non-git-archive (hard error) + a cross-repo turn leaving the target tree free of relay-system/. Costly containment-kernel change; GH-30 marker comment." },

./relay-system/2026-06-27/p51-design-080020/p51-design.codex.md:685:    56	- **Dueling/relay · commit-signal advance (file-driven mode)** 🟢 — **built + tested 2026-06-26** (agy review pending): `poll.sh --turn-source file` reads whose-turn from the relay `NEXT:` field, making the tick token **optional** — exactly what the first cross-repo dueling run needed when the Maintainer Claude worked straight off the relay file and never joined `tick`. Optional `--peer-commit-repo`/`--peer-commit-match` add the "advance on the peer's fix commit" signal (idle → run-runner only once a matching commit lands). `relay_field` now tolerates `**bold**` markdown keys; STATUS terminal + artifact-scope-clean still apply. 7 new assertions in [test/poll-driver.sh](test/poll-driver.sh); `validate.sh` **48/48**. → [poll.sh](relay-automation/poll.sh) · [Field findings](PROJECT/2-WORKING/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./relay-system/2026-06-27/p51-design-080020/p51-design.codex.md:686:    57	- **Dueling/relay · token resilience for a non-participating peer + multi-round reuse** 🟡 — **core addressed by `--turn-source file`** (token-optional ⇒ no `parked suspect` stall and no `spent/reserved — not claimable` mid-run, the two lock failures from 2026-06-26). Residual (only when you DO use the tick token): a non-watchdog `reap`+`claim` reconcile path (still manual) and token-reuse semantics that survive N `release --to peer` round-trips. Lower priority now that file-source is the recommended dueling path. → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/2-WORKING/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./relay-system/2026-06-27/p51-design-080020/p51-design.codex.md:687:    58	- **Gate design · convergence gates that pin a *worse* code shape** 🐞 — the KWFS `#70` gate statically extracts a literal `return array(...)` from *each* of three formatter methods and asserts equality, which makes the issue's intended fix (one canonical method + delegate/delete the duplicates) **fail the gate** — the Maintainer was forced to keep three duplicated-but-identical literals, divergence the gate calls "converged." Generalizes: static-extraction needle gates can pin a less-maintainable shape than the refactor they exist to enforce. Fix direction: assert runtime output-key equality (or canonical + delegation), not three literals. → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/2-WORKING/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)
./relay-system/2026-06-27/p51-design-080020/p51-design.codex.md:688:    59	- **Orchestration · in-loop gate verification must run sandbox-off** 🟡 — an orchestrator/watcher re-running a target gate (`bash tests/run.sh`) under the Claude Bash sandbox **false-fails** (PHP can't create lock files → bogus "syntax errors"); the peer's un-sandboxed run is authoritative. Handle: in-loop gate self-checks run sandbox-off, or trust the peer's run + spot-confirm sandbox-off (memory: `codex-cli-needs-sandbox-disabled` family). → [AUTOMATED-RELAY.md → Field findings](PROJECT/2-WORKING/AUTOMATED-RELAY.md#field-findings--first-cross-repo-dueling-run-2026-06-26)

./relay-system/2026-06-29/gh40-codeqa.md:82:- [Should] `relay-automation/proposals-sink.sh:44-54` reports success even when the append itself fails. The grouped append uses `>> "$TARGET"` without checking its exit status; if the parent dir is missing or the file is unwritable, bash returns a redirection error, then the script still prints `appended N proposal(s)` and exits 0. That makes the sink lie about having preserved the Reviewer’s proposals. Fix: gate the append in an `if ! { ...; } >>"$TARGET"; then ...; exit 1; fi` block and only emit the success line after a verified write; add a negative test in `test/phase3-signoff-guard.sh` for an unwritable or nonexistent target path.
./relay-system/2026-06-29/gh40-codeqa.md:93:- [Should] silent append failure — **Implemented.** First tried gating on the redirect's exit status, but confirmed your point further: a failed redirect on a *negated group command* does **not** propagate non-zero on bash 3.2 (it printed the error yet exited 0). Switched to an explicit pre-check: target dir must exist + be writable (or the existing file writable) before appending, else exit 1 with no success line. New regression test (nonexistent dir → fails, no false success).
./relay-system/2026-06-27/kwfs-73-codex-qa.md:38:The fix replaces that with a single-pass bulk rebuild: `rebuild_all()` streams shop_coupon rows from wp_posts in ID-ordered chunks; `bulk_upsert_posts()` writes one multi-row `INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` using the existing `normalize_code()/normalize_text()` for search parity. Backfill + Builder were rewired to use it; the builder runs it as one pass (done=true, no cron fan-out). Hardening added replace-state (clear stale rows, blog-scoped) and fail-loud (a `failed` flag; a non-empty batch that writes 0 rows is treated as a failed bulk write, not a completed run).

./AUDIT/relay-automation-transcripts/recommendations-studio-alignment.md:25:- Definition of Done: **The Scenario Studio reads as part of the same page as the plain-English rec cards: (a) shared vocabulary/altitude — levers lead with plain-English names (technical alias kept as a secondary tag), not raw-algorithm jargon; (b) visible lever→card linkage — each lever names the cards it governs, and the preview note names the affected card; (c) honest gating — the Studio is marked an internal admin/support-view surface (it only renders in support view in the real app); (d) the two "commit" verbs are disambiguated — Studio "Save as live" (engine tuning) vs the per-card "Mark as implemented" (activation log); (e) NO misstatement of the engine — the cross-sell "affinity floor" (a 0–1 co-purchase score) and the "≥20-co-orders" deploy tier are RELATED BUT DISTINCT gates; the copy must say "works alongside", not equate them; (f) no WCAG AA regression.**

If you discover you need to edit files outside your declared paths, expand scope **before** editing:\\n   ```\\n   ./experiments/coordination-layer/bin/tick scope <TASK-ID> --agent gemini \\\\\\n     --paths \\"<expanded,globs>\\"\\n   ```\\n\\n5. Complete the task with one of:\\n   ```\\n   # success:\\n   ./experiments/coordination-layer/bin/tick done <TASK-ID> --agent gemini\\n\\n   # stuck (failing tests after multiple attempts, infinite loop, etc.):\\n   ./experiments/coordination-layer/bin/tick break <TASK-ID> --agent gemini \\\\\\n     --reason \\"<short reason>\\"\\n\\n   # hand off to a specific peer:\\n   ./experiments/coordination-layer/bin/tick release <TASK-ID> --agent gemini \\\\\\n     --to <codex|claude>\\n   ```\\n\\n## You are being measured on\\n\\nAfter the session, `tick analyze` will report:\\n- Did you claim before editing? (any work commit not inside an active claim by you = **unclaimed-work violation**)\\n- Did your declared paths match your actual edits? (any commit touching files outside your claim\'s globs = **drift**)\\n- Did you use `tick scope` / `tick done` / `tick break` appropriately?\\n\\nHonest narrow declaration matters more than ambitious wide scope. If you only need `src/billing/charge.js`, declare exactly that — don\'t pre-claim `src/**` \\"just in case\\".\\n\\n## Hard rules\\n\\n- If `tick claim` returns `lost:`, you do not own the task. Run `tick next` again. Do not edit files for a task you did not win.\\n- Declare every glob you intend to touch. Use `tick scope` to expand mid-task; do not silently edit outside declared scope.\\n- `claim` / `scope` / `release` / `break` / `done` all auto-commit and push. If push fails twice, abort and pick a different task — do not retry indefinitely.\\n- Critical events fetch+rebase before pushing. Don\'t manually fight rebases; if you hit a conflict the tool didn\'t resolve, abort and surface it.\\n\\n## When you\'re done\\n\\nWhen you\'ve completed your assigned tasks (or `tick next` returns `(no available task)`), tell Noel: \\"I\'m done — N tasks completed, M circuit-broken, K released.\\" Wait for his go-ahead before exiting.\\n--- End of Context from: /private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/consult-wt-23743-8626/GEMINI.md ---\\n</project_context>\\n</loaded_context>\\n</session_context>"}]},{"role":"user","parts":[{"text":"You are an INDEPENDENT advisor in a one-shot cross-model consult. ./relay-system/2026-07-03/gh102-qa-082014/gh102-qa.codex.md:1032:    80	- **Gate design · convergence gates that pin a *worse* code shape** 🐞 — the KWFS `#70` gate statically extracts a literal `return array(...)` from *each* of three formatter methods and asserts equality, which makes the issue's intended fix (one canonical method + delegate/delete the duplicates) **fail the gate** — the Maintainer was forced to keep three duplicated-but-identical literals, divergence the gate calls "converged." Also trigger if the user asks to set up a check-in loop between a local model and a frontier model for a long-running task."

./AUDIT/relay-automation-transcripts/agents-md-bq-governance.md:84:- [Should] AGENTS.md:52 — Missing partition/cluster pruning as the primary cost reduction mechanism. The 10 rules mention partitions only as an aside in Rule 2, but do not establish partition/cluster key filtering in the `WHERE` clause as the absolute primary lever for reducing billed bytes. Proposed fix: Add a new Rule 2b: `2b. **Mandate Partition & Cluster Filters**: When querying large partitioned or clustered tables, you must include the partition/cluster key in your WHERE clause (e.g., WHERE event_date >= CURRENT_DATE() - 7). This is the primary mechanism to prune blocks and reduce billed bytes scanned.`
./AUDIT/relay-automation-transcripts/agents-md-bq-governance.md:88:- [Pass] AGENTS.md:31-50 — Checked the mandatory import and sync gold-standard SOP. The workflow is robustly designed to introduce deliberate friction (requiring a checklist based on `utils/wp-bq/IMPORT-TEMPLATE.md` at `temp/<site>/imports/<SITE>-IMPORT-YYYY-MM-DD.md`) before any irreversible production write, protecting MySQL-to-BQ migration flows.
./AUDIT/relay-automation-transcripts/agents-md-bq-governance.md:101:- [Should] Rule 5 join-order folklore (@ AGENTS.md:60, Gemini cited :63) — **Implemented** → rewrote as Rule 6 "Join order is auto-optimized — don't hand-order tables"; removed the "largest table first" advice, redirected to pre-filtering join keys / no `CROSS JOIN` / partition filters in join conditions. · Proof: textual only (correct — BQ's cost-based optimizer reorders joins; syntactic order is not a cost lever).

./AUDIT/relay-automation-transcripts/consult-dogfood2.codex.md:1237:    13	`tick` is a tiny CLI backed by an event log under `.tick/events/`. Each event is a separate JSONL file (one event per file = disjoint files = zero merge conflicts). `tick project` folds events into `.tick/STATE.md`. Coordination is **local-transport** (since Run 2): every verb is a pure append to a shared local `.tick/events/` — no git push or fetch per event. Peer agents see each other's events by reading the same shared directory, and a per-repo `O_EXCL` lock (`withClaimLock`, under `.tick/locks/`) serialises concurrent claims into a real mutex. `git` is used only incidentally — `tick` locates the repo root via `git rev-parse`, and `tick reap` defaults its actor name from `git config user.name`. Coordination state and `tick analyze` read only `.tick/`.

./relay-system/2026-07-04/hq-gh-128-code-review.md:67:- [Blocker] [utils/hq/hq-lib.sh](/private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/rtl-wt.xNouMl/utils/hq/hq-lib.sh:159) trusts any non-empty `XYZ_PATH` without checking that it exists, and [utils/hq/hq.sh](/private/var/folders/69/3l_82qtj7fzglnt_jjg07jh40000gn/T/rtl-wt.xNouMl/utils/hq/hq.sh:347) treats that as sufficient for Tier A. Repro: a stale XYZ row pointing at a deleted repo still made `bash utils/hq/hq.sh fire --gh-issue 9 --risk 2 stale-app` print `GATES PASS` and emit `swarm-preflight.sh --target-root <missing path>`. On a degraded registry, `fire` should refuse or fall back, not arm dispatch at a dead target. Fix: validate `XYZ_PATH` before returning it, degrade to filesystem lookup when possible, and require an existing repo path before Tier A / `fire` passes.

./relay-system/2026-07-03/gh41-terminality-review-094117/gh41-terminality-review.codex.md:205:2. **One canonical event log; every surface is a projection.** `tick` accretes events; `STATE.md` is the current projection. Reads go through the projection; writes go through a `claim/take/scope/done` verb. Nothing canonical lives in two places where it can drift. ./relay-system/2026-07-03/gh41-terminality-review-094117/gh41-terminality-review.codex.md:464:   101	    // a displaced writer from redirecting the reservation to an accomplice.

./relay-system/2026-07-03/gh41-terminality-review-094117/gh41-terminality-review.codex.md:734:**Decision:** Add a monotonic, per-task `epoch` to the `tick` event schema (bump `schema_version` 0.1.0 → 0.2.0) and make the projection kernel (`src/project.js` `fold`) **fence** any mutating event whose epoch is below the current owner's. A displaced writer — even one that revives with the **same agent id** — can no longer advance (`done`/`circuit_break`), corrupt (`scope_changed`), retire (`released`), or redirect the handoff of a task it no longer owns. Rejected events are recorded in a deterministic audit log (`.tick/rejected.jsonl`, surfaced by `tick fences`).

./relay-system/2026-07-04/gh48-zone-design-review-074511/gh48-zone-design-review.codex.md:857:ROADMAP.md:81:- **GH-30 · optional centralized transcript archive** ✅ **ALL PHASES SHIPPED 2026-07-03 (Plan A lane 5 complete)** — optional `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` redirects relay/consult/marathon transcripts out of each foreign repo's `relay-system/` into one namespaced archive; default unset = byte-for-byte current. **Phases 1–2:** single resolver `rtl_transcript_root` + `rtl_repo_slug` in `relay-turn-lib.sh` (Model-A validation: absolute + exists + git repo, else HARD ERROR), and all writers derive their transcript base from it. **Phase 3 (Model A, risk-4 core):** `rtl_init` flags `RTL_ARCHIVE_MODE` when the relay file's git repo ≠ `RTL_ROOT`, and `rtl_enforce` commits the **transcript into the archive** via an isolated `git -C` pathspec commit while the **code artifact + `.tick` token stay on the target** — target tree free of `relay-system/`, no transcript commit in target history, and the isolated archive commit can never orphan a concurrent peer commit (GH-13 guard target-only, holds when token-tree ≠ transcript-tree). **Phase 4:** `extract-relay-telemetry.sh` reads the resolver, aggregating across all `<repo-slug>/` dirs. **Phase 5:** `new-relay.sh` wired; `CONSUMING.md` + `README.md` document the contract; CHANGELOG bet recorded. New `test/archive-commit.sh` (16) + `test/archive-telemetry.sh` (3) → `validate.sh` green. cx/risk/eff 3/4/3. codex
**ANSWER**

The rename to `ate` is mostly applied correctly on the user-facing/install surfaces, but I would not treat this skill as unattended-safe yet. The biggest remaining problem is that `run_variations.py` can still crash on non-network LM Studio response failures, and the doc/workflow around `error_log.jsonl` and `control.json` is looser than the implementation.

**FINDINGS**

- [Blocker] `ask_gemma()` still has whole-run crash paths after the network call succeeds. `requests.exceptions.RequestException` is now caught, but `resp.json()["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]` can still raise on malformed JSON or an unexpected response shape, which would terminate a 2-3hr run on a single bad LM Studio reply. [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:45) (lines 45-57)

- [Should] The check-in / rollup workflow is easy to point at the wrong files. `run_variations.py`, `checkin.py`, and `compile_issue.py` all default to relative `error_log.jsonl` / `control.json` paths, but the quick start launches the worker in the scratch repo and then later shows bare `checkin.py` / `compile_issue.py` invocations without re-stating “run these from the same cwd or pass explicit paths.” That can silently produce an empty summary or write `control.json` in the wrong place. [SKILL.md](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/SKILL.md:39) (lines 39-67), [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:146) (lines 146-147), [checkin.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/checkin.py:60) (lines 60-69), [compile_issue.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/compile_issue.py:73) (lines 73-95)

- [Should] One of the documented drift checks is not implemented. `SKILL.md` says to treat “no new lines appended for 2+ check-in cycles” as a hang signal, but `checkin.py` has no persisted state, no mtime tracking, and no previous-offset memory, so it cannot detect lack of progress across separate invocations. [SKILL.md](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/SKILL.md:79) (lines 79-80), [checkin.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/checkin.py:17) (lines 17-55)

- [Should] The worker’s actual execution model contradicts the “untested variation” / “variations exhausted” docs and makes results stateful. The code uses `itertools.cycle(combos)` and only stops on time budget or a safety cap, while the docs describe moving through untested combinations and possibly ending when the YAML is exhausted. Combined with the explicit “no cleanup/reset” note, later iterations are affected by prior edits and duplicates are expected. [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:167) (lines 167-210), [SKILL.md](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/SKILL.md:15) (lines 15-18, 62-67, 127-128)

- [Should] Timeout handling is not process-tree-safe. `subprocess.run(..., timeout=...)` will stop the direct `aider` process, but if `aider` leaves child processes behind they can survive and accumulate across a long unattended run. That is a real robustness gap for a multi-hour harness. [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:109) (lines 109-121)

**ANSWER**

The rename to `ate` is mostly applied correctly on the user-facing/install surfaces, but I would not treat this skill as unattended-safe yet. The biggest remaining problem is that `run_variations.py` can still crash on non-network LM Studio response failures, and the doc/workflow around `error_log.jsonl` and `control.json` is looser than the implementation.

**FINDINGS**

- [Blocker] `ask_gemma()` still has whole-run crash paths after the network call succeeds. `requests.exceptions.RequestException` is now caught, but `resp.json()["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]` can still raise on malformed JSON or an unexpected response shape, which would terminate a 2-3hr run on a single bad LM Studio reply. [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:45) (lines 45-57)

- [Should] The check-in / rollup workflow is easy to point at the wrong files. `run_variations.py`, `checkin.py`, and `compile_issue.py` all default to relative `error_log.jsonl` / `control.json` paths, but the quick start launches the worker in the scratch repo and then later shows bare `checkin.py` / `compile_issue.py` invocations without re-stating “run these from the same cwd or pass explicit paths.” That can silently produce an empty summary or write `control.json` in the wrong place. [SKILL.md](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/SKILL.md:39) (lines 39-67), [run_variations.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py:146) (lines 146-147), [checkin.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/checkin.py:60) (lines 60-69), [compile_issue.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/compile_issue.py:73) (lines 73-95)

- [Should] One of the documented drift checks is not implemented. `SKILL.md` says to treat “no new lines appended for 2+ check-in cycles” as a hang signal, but `checkin.py` has no persisted state, no mtime tracking, and no previous-offset memory, so it cannot detect lack of progress across separate invocations. [SKILL.md](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/SKILL.md:79) (lines 79-80), [checkin.py](/private/var/folders/dh/ypr5rkgn3b9fzlzlmj69yd_r0000gn/T/consult-wt-70265-4593/utils/ate/scripts/checkin.py:17) (lines 17-55)

- [Should] The worker’s actual execution model contradicts the “untested variation” / “variations exhausted” docs and makes results stateful. The code uses `itertools.cycle(combos)` and only stops on time budget or a safety cap, while the docs describe moving through untested combinations and possibly ending when the YAML is exhausted. Combined with the explicit “no cleanup/reset” note, later iterations are affected by prior edits and duplicates are expected. - [Should] Timeout handling is not process-tree-safe. `subprocess.run(..., timeout=...)` will stop the direct `aider` process, but if `aider` leaves child processes behind they can survive and accumulate across a long unattended run.
